- If each block has 2 TMs: 4 TMs total
- If one block has 1 and other has 2: 3 TMs total

- The helix should not have charged residues (D, E, K, R, H) in the middle

Let me make my final decision. The key question is whether each hydrophobic block contains 1 or 2 TMs.

Arguments for 2 TMs per block (total 4):
- Each block is 30-34 residues, which is long for a single TM (typical 18-25)
- There are identifiable breaks (D-S in block 1, Y-P or E in block 2)
- Sub-blocks have very high hydrophobicity (average &gt; 2.0)

Arguments for 1 TM per block (total 2, not an option):
- The polar interruptions are short (2-3 residues)
- Some TM helices can be longer than 25 residues

Arguments for 3 TMs:
- Block 1 splits into 2 TMs (15 + 19 residues)
- Block 2 stays as 1 TM (30 residues)
- 30 is long but within range for a tilted helix

Wait, actually 14-15 residues can span a thin membrane but not a typical bilayer. The hydrophobic core of a typical lipid bilayer is about 30 Å, and an alpha-helix rises about 1.5 Å per residue, so you need at least 20 residues (30/1.5 = 20) to span it perpendicular. At a tilt of 30°, you&#x27;d need about 23 residues.

So 14-15 residues would NOT span the membrane. This means block 2 is likely ONE TM helix (30 residues, possibly tilted).

For block 1:
- Sub-block A (21-35): 15 residues - this is also borderline/short for spanning
- Sub-block B (36-54): 19 residues - this can span with a slight tilt

If sub-block A is too short to span, then block 1 is also ONE TM helix, giving only 2 total TMs (not an option).

It&#x27;s part of the N-DRC (nexin-dynein regulatory complex) which is essential for flagellar function. The protein is expressed during spermiogenesis/spermatid differentiation when the flagellum is being assembled.

The key distinction here is:
- Meiotic nuclear division (D) occurs before the axoneme is assembled - it&#x27;s the division of chromosomes in spermatocytes
- Spermatid differentiation (A) is the process of transforming round spermatids into mature spermatozoa, which includes flagellar assembly
- Sperm capacitation (B) occurs after the sperm is fully formed

Since SPAG16 is an axonemal protein involved in flagellar assembly, it is most directly involved in spermatid differentiation (spermiogenesis), where the sperm flagellum is built.

The answer should be A. Spermatid differentiation.
